=== PROMPT VARIANT ===

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an integer n which represents an array nums containing the numbers from 1 to n in order. Additionally, you are given a 2D array conflictingPairs, where conflictingPairs[i] = [a, b] indicates that a and b form a conflicting pair.
Remove exactly one element from conflictingPairs. Afterward, count the number of non-empty subarrays of nums which do not contain both a and b for any remaining conflicting pair [a, b].
Return the maximum number of subarrays possible after removing exactly one conflicting pair.
 
Example 1:

Input: n = 4, conflictingPairs = [[2,3],[1,4]]
Output: 9
Explanation:

Remove [2, 3] from conflictingPairs. Now, conflictingPairs = [[1, 4]].
There are 9 subarrays in nums where [1, 4] do not appear together. They are [1], [2], [3], [4], [1, 2], [2, 3], [3, 4], [1, 2, 3] and [2, 3, 4].
The maximum number of subarrays we can achieve after removing one element from conflictingPairs is 9.


Example 2:

Input: n = 5, conflictingPairs = [[1,2],[2,5],[3,5]]
Output: 12
Explanation:

Remove [1, 2] from conflictingPairs. Now, conflictingPairs = [[2, 5], [3, 5]].
There are 12 subarrays in nums where [2, 5] and [3, 5] do not appear together.
The maximum number of subarrays we can achieve after removing one element from conflictingPairs is 12.


 
Constraints:

2 <= n <= 10^5
1 <= conflictingPairs.length <= 2 * n
conflictingPairs[i].length == 2
1 <= conflictingPairs[i][j] <= n
conflictingPairs[i][0] != conflictingPairs[i][1]

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def maxSubarrays(self, n: int, conflictingPairs: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Next greater or equal to the right:
def compute_next_ge(arr):
    n=len(arr)-1
    nge=[n+1]*(n+1)
    stack=[]  # indices with increasing? We'll test.
    for i in range(n,0,-1):
        while stack and arr[stack[-1]] < arr[i]:
            stack.pop()
        if stack:
            nge[i]=stack[-1]
        else:
            nge[i]=n+1
        stack.append(i)
    return nge

top1 = 0
top2 = 0
top1_arr = [0]*(n+1)
top2_arr = [0]*(n+1)
prefTop2 = [0]*(n+1)
for r in range(1,n+1):
    for x in groups[r]:
        # update top1, top2
        if x > top1:
            top2 = top1
            top1 = x
        elif x == top1:
            top2 = x  # because another max
        elif x > top2:
            top2 = x
        # else no change
    top1_arr[r] = top1
    top2_arr[r] = top2
    prefTop2[r] = prefTop2[r-1] + top2
    # sumM += top1
Need be careful: If x > top1, top2 = old top1. But what if old top1=0 and top2 maybe? If top1=0, top2=0, add first x>0 => top2=0. Good. If top1=5, top2=5 (multiple max), add x=6 > top1 => top2=5.         cnt = 0

                cnt += 1
        cntMax[y] = cnt

Let's derive reduction formula one more time from top2 perspective to ensure no off-by-one.

Original M(r) = top1_all(r). For candidate pair p with value v at y. For r in [y, T-1], p is the unique maximum among active pairs, so top1_all(r)=v. Wait top2_all(r) is second largest among all active pairs including p. Since p is largest unique, second largest among all active pairs is exactly the largest among remaining pairs. Pseudo:

from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maxSubarrays(self, n: int, conflictingPairs: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        groups = [[] for _ in range(n+1)]
        for a,b in conflictingPairs:
            x = a if a < b else b
            y = b if a < b else a
            groups[y].append(x)
        
        val = [0]*(n+1)
        cnt_max = [0]*(n+1)
        for y in range(1,n+1):
            lst = groups[y]
            if lst:
                mx = max(lst)
                val[y] = mx
                cnt = 0
                for x in lst:
                    if x == mx:
                        cnt += 1
                cnt_max[y] = cnt
        
        top1 = 0
        top2 = 0
        top1_arr = [0]*(n+1)
        pref_top2 = [0]*(n+1)
        sum_m = 0
        for r in range(1,n+1):
            for x in groups[r]:
                if x > top1:
                    top2 = top1
                    top1 = x
                elif x == top1:
                    top2 = x
                elif x > top2:
                    top2 = x
            top1_arr[r] = top1
            pref_top2[r] = pref_top2[r-1] + top2
            sum_m += top1
        
        next_ge = [n+1]*(n+1)
        stack = []
        for i in range(n,0,-1):
            vi = val[i]
            while stack and val[stack[-1]] < vi:
                stack.pop()
            if stack:
                next_ge[i] = stack[-1]
            else:
                next_ge[i] = n+1
            stack.append(i)
        
        max_reduction = 0
        for y in range(1,n+1):
            if val[y] == 0:
                continue
            if cnt_max[y] != 1:
                continue
            if val[y] <= top1_arr[y-1]:
                continue
            v = val[y]
            t = next_ge[y]
            # t is first > y with val >= v, or n+1
            reduction = (t - y) * v - (pref_top2[t-1] - pref_top2[y-1])
            if reduction > max_reduction:
                max_reduction = reduction
        
        total = n * (n+1) // 2
        return total - sum_m + max_reduction
